Here I would apply[^008-note69] [with this leaf begins part of the application preached only the 2nd time, at commencement.] this Exhortation in particular to those members of the College in this Place who are expecting this week to receive the Honour of the society

______

The doctrine that has been now insisted on is in a peculiar manner proper for your Consideration who are now in your youth & who are have been Educated have here had so much expended ar upon you to set you for publick in your Education to fit you for publick service in the Θ & who are now going forth about going forth into the Θ from under the immediate[^008-note70] Care & Inspection of those who have been your Instructors & Guides . You especially stand in need of a Counsellor & at in a peculiar manner at such a Juncture perhaps beyond all others that ever happened in the Course of your Lives.

_____

you are yet in your you are now in your youth an age wherein Persons do especially need the Counsel in order to their being steerd clear of the snares the many snares by which Persons are exposed to ruin especially in a at that age you are now going from [^008-note71] under the Care of such as have been heretofore your faithful Guides & Counsellers & have had the Can of whose Constant direction & Inspection you have had the Benefit .

_______

But miserable will you be if henceforward you should be left to your selves without any Counseller or Guide

_______

especially when it is Considered that you are going forth into a Θ full of snares dangers & snares that is like a great wilderness full of Pits & drought & fiery flying serpents full of those snares[^008-note72] which prove the Ruin of the greater part of mankind

______

and your need of a Counseller [is] beyond others in that you have been educated for Pu publick Business of greater Importance than what usual one any of those any tha the greater part of mankind . & most of you have probably have been designed for the great & arduous & sacred work of the gospel ministry . a work

______

If you are wise & considerate Persons it will be natural for you under your Present Circumstances sensible of [^008-note73] your own Infirmity & darkness & weakness it will be natural for you under your present Circumstances to say in your Hearts what shall I do for a wise & faithful Guide to whom shall I repair for Counsel to direct my steps & regulate my conduct in the Θ

________

I have you have now heard of the great Counseller The most eminent & excellent Guide that ever was or ever will be .

__________

I would now therefore being however unworthy one of the messengers of this Great shepherd & Leader of mankind & being[^008-note74] in his Providence brought hither at this time to this to this Place & Called to speak in his at this Time in his name I would now advise you to repair to this great Counseller look to Him for & with & let all your Heart & soul look to Him & adhere to him as you[r] Guide to Lead y you in every step of your Conduct in the Θ

____________

dont Rest any of you without Good Evidence of Interest in Him & good & Clear Evidence that this glorious Counseller & Light of the Θ is your Lord & Redeemer your shepherd . your Light & salvation[^008-note75] Persons in your Circumstances in a special manner will be miserable if you are Aliens & strangers without any Interest in aliens & strangers without X here Children of darkness sheep without a shepherd without X & so without G. in the Θ. sorrow fill'd the Hearts of the 11 discip. when X was about to Leave em as to his Human Presence and indeed they would have been miserable & Comfortless if X had forsaken them as to his spiritual & gracious Presence .[^008-note76] But He told them He would not leave em comfortless He would come to em by his sp. & be[-] in them & dwell with Them as in the 14 Chap. of Joh. which well might support which might well support Them & fill Them with unspeakable Comfort.

______________

Happy indeed will you [be] if now you are going forth into the Θ you have He who is the Light of the Θ takes up his abode in your Heart. arises as a blessed day star there a Liv founta a Constant living Fountain of Light in your soul. to enlighten you set in all your[^008-note77] way through this dark Θ to be your Light in the dark valley of the shad . of death & to be your Light in that world where the saints shall not stand in need of the Light of the sun ------ because the Glo. of the L. G. & the Lamb. is their everlasting Light. that [will] never set & never be clouded or eclipsed. But unless X do unless X doe you are savingly instructed & enlightened by X. all you the Learning you have Got in the seminary of Learning in this Place and all that the Knowl. that ever you are like to obtain will prove in pr vain to you. in vain as y to you own Happiness instead of that it will but aggravate your Guilt here & eternal mis. hereafter and it will probably be in vain as to your any usefull real usefullness to yo the Θ the Θ of mankind. has your Talents &[^008-note78] capacities will be the greater but your disposition not the better but probably the worse & if it should be so your greater Talents will in Effect be only greater advantages in your Hands to do mischief in the Θ

there is no work in the Θ wherein men stand in in such great & manifold necessity of X as there teacher as that last mention'd without this men are in the utmost danger of eternally und ruining thems. & others that are led by em when the blind leads the Blind nothing else can be expected than that both

### 934. I Pet. 3:19-20

### 934. I Pet. 3'19-20 (6_49)

Jonathan Edwards

### 11.Is. 35'8(ed.).xml

Scripture

### VII. and VIII.

1 Peter iii. 19, 20-> "By which also he went and preached to the spirits in prison," &c...*

Two things it is my present purpose to observe concerning the spirits or souls of those wicked men that Noah preached to... 1. How long ago they lived. 2. How those souls are here spoken of as to their present state, "spirits in prison."

DoctrineThose wicked men who lived before the flood, and went to Hell in Noah’s time, are there still. 1. I would give some reasons why they have remained in Hell so long. 2. Observe in some respects in what circumstances they remain there all this while. I. Give some reasons. *Negatively* 1. ’Tis not because Hell has been tolerable to ’em. 2. ’Tis not because they ben’t convinced of their former folly,[^015-note1] that they have not yet been brought to their right mind or to their

<!-- p. 204 -->

judgment of things. 3. ’Tis not because they are careless about their own welfare and ease, and not willing to be at any possible labours or cost in order to escape, if there were any hope of deliverance. Not because they don’t wish and long...not thoroughly overwhelmed...not because they ben’t in earnest...that they ben’t thoroughly engaged in their spirits...not yet disposed to own that the courses that they went on going weren’t good...not disposed to attend to any offer of mercy...because they begrudge...would be unwilling to lay out themselves...not because they are covetous...not willing to be shewn... 4. ’Tis not because they were weaker than the generality of men. Many of them were giants. 5. Not because there is not a great number of those who are disposed to unite their strength to burst prison. *Affirmatively* 1. Their souls are immortal...made for eternity...of a nature agreeable to this design... Thought they desire it, they can’t return to nothing. "Seek death and cannot find it." Extreme torment, but no tendency to annihilate the soul...tendency to sink it, but not to reduce it to nothing. ’Tis not with the soul as ’tis with the body in its present mortal state...extreme oppression tends to destroy it. 2. They are not in a state of probation. 3. Their debt is what they can’t pay... Great debt to Divine justice. Have not wherewith... Nothing to pay... Cast into prison till they should pay the last mite... 4. THERE IS NO GOSPEL PREACHED IN HELL. Christ did not die for the damned... Had no respect to that world... to those in this state... any more than the devil. No means of grace. Means of grace not accommodated to that state. No manner of provision made in any respect for their relief. No aid. Preaching of the Word don’t reach them. The prayers of saints, of godly friends, don’t reach them. 5. The place and state they are in was never designed for the exercise of mercy. God is infinitely merciful; but the exercises of mercy are not for ever... Limited by Wisdom in various respects: has declared what the purposes of His wisdom are with respect to the exercises of His mercy. As to the kind of beings; fallen men and not angels... His declarations are according to truth.. His purposes are not altered.. Hence no mercy in Hell.. Thought their pain is extreme.. God don’t pity ’em. Though their wishes for deliverance are great...thought their cries are loud... though long continued... though it be exceeding intolerable. 6. By being longer in Hell, they have not become any more fit for any other state...don’t make ’em better...although indeed their judgments are convinced, yet their hearts are the same. No

<!-- p. 205 -->

change in their dispositions. There are no conversions in Hell. The wrath makes a great change indeed; but no saving change. The wicked in Noah’s days were most of them very wicked on earth, yet, in some respects, they became worse when they went to Hell. Not fit for Heaven. Not fit to come and live in this world. Fit for no other place. That is the place provided and fitted for such. 7. The prison is strong that holds them. Delivered up into the hands of Satan...against whom they have no strength. And his cruelty is as strong as his power. And what is much more than devils, God confines them...irreversible sentence binds them. Omniscience opposes them. No escape by subtlety unobserved. Often here, in this world, persons break prison by stealth...get away by night, when no one observes them...And God’s Almighty power holds ’em down. And God ever lives. He is unchangeable. He never will alter His purpose...never will forget His own Son...never will forget His righteous sentence... II. Observe, in several things, in what circumstances they have been in, all this so long a time. 1. They have not been in a state of insensibility. "The Rich man." Not less their sensibility by long-continued torment and misery...hence not been deprived of the use of reason. 2. Have had no time of respite all this while. No rest. Rev. xiv. 11-"No rest day nor night." Both day and night are mentioned, because the night in this world is a time of rest. Have never been asleep...never find any resting-place. Never find out any assuaging medicines...any cool shadows...cooling green...never found a drop of water...never found any expedient for mitigating their torment. They have had much to goad ’em, in exercising their invention. Necessity is the mother of invention. But their inventions have not saved them...have had nothing to divert them...no amusement to take off their attention... 3. Have not forgotten things that were so long ago, or when they were on earth. "Son, remember."..Will remember over all at the Day of Judgment. Have not forgotten pleasant circumstances they lived in...swam in delights...their lives were long...the wickedness they committed...the opportunities they had...the warnings...Noah’s preaching...their folly...they thoughts they had concerning God and His testimony. 4. Their misery is not grown more tolerable by their being so long used to it. 5. The great additions that have been made to the numbers from their time has been no comfort to ’em. 6. They have had no hope all this while...have none now...though their torments have been so long continued. This is verified concerning all that go down to the pit of Hell. (Isa. xxxviii. 19)- "They that go down to the pit cannot hope for Thy truth."

Use

<!-- p. 206 -->

May be of warning: Let sinners in these days take warning. Those that now live in unbelief and impenitence are in danger of the same. 1. Let me call upon you seriously to consider how long a time those who have been spoken of have already worn out themselves in Hell: they have been there 4000 years. How many ages? How many and great change, and successions, and transactions? How many generations have passed? How many successions of those who had come into being, gradually grown up, and grown old, and then died? Not only have many generations gradually come upon the stage and have died; but many great and populous nations have come into being, and have flourished, and made a great figure in the world for many ages, and then by degrees have dwindled and wasted, swallowed up by other nations and come to nothing, and nothing of them now known but by history. And some of them very ancient and powerful, so that even the very history of them is almost come to nothing and vanished; and all since they have been suffering the flames of Hell, without any cessation or rest. The world has been peopled...subdued...many mighty princes have appeared and made a good bustle, and none...whose memory is now almost forgotten...Very great and magnificent cities...and not ’tis hardly to be known where they stood. Many kingdoms, yea, many great monarchies...Those four monarchies of the world. How many things have worn out! The strongest fortunes...strongest empires...most durable monuments...pillars of brass... monuments of marble stone...Languages used as the common speech of large countries. Such things have gradually perished, and length of time worn ’em out; but yet the torments of the "spirits in prison" yet remains...not come to nothing...are as fresh and lively and in as great vigor as the first moment. The souls who are the subjects of these miseries were sinners, and their miseries immortal. Since they have been in Hell there has been a long series of wonderful dispensations of God towards His Church and the whole world. After the flood, the nations degenerated... fell off to idolatry...Abraham was "called"...children of Jacob became a great nation...Christ was foretold of old, from time to time...anciently prefigured...and in the fulness of time Christ actually came into the world...all these ancient prophecies and ancient types were fulfilled...the Jews, God’s ancient people, rejected...Gentiles called...prophecies were given...a mighty change made in the Roman kingdom...Antichrist arose...reigned for a long time...but at length the Reformation came...During all those mighty changes on earth those souls spoken of in the doctrine, continued without ease day or night or one minute’s respite, wrestling with the mighty torments of Hell; and so they continue still...torment not abated. These great and mighty changes on earth have not affected them to cause and change there. There have been many good times on earth: days

<!-- p. 207 -->

of great good; but... 2. How we may suppose the things which they remember of their past lives now affects them...their worldly enjoyments... the length of time of their past ease and pleasures...they lived long lives...their past opportunities...their long warnings...the preaching they had...their folly and stupidity...obstinacy... 3. How many since have followed their steps and have gone to Hell in like manner. ’Tis not the manner of men to take warning by those that perish before them...Those that are gone before can see the folly of those that come after, whom it will do no good. The rich man in Hell. Those that went to Hell in Noah’s day, see the folly of those that come to ’em from generation to generation. But those that follow after don’t see the folly of those who went before, and so they follow them... 4. They that have worn out so many ages in Hell, are never the nearer any end of their misery. The time is very long that they have suffered...many tedious days...tedious years...and tedious ages one after another...The must wear out another space of time as long as this in Hell flames, and another after that....Soon the time will come when they will actually have worn out a million times such as...yea, a million and millions...God foresees the time...knows the particular passage of the day. Yea, as many such ages as there are particles of dust on the globe of the earth...And even then NO MERCY. It will be without end. FOR EVER, YEA, FOR EVER. Therefore take warning. If you neglect to take warning you will go to Hell...and the time will come when you will have been as long as they now have been...and you must also be there millions of millions...and you will be no nearer...yea, will be in absolute despair as they are... your wishes, and cries, and strength, and entreaties will be in vain. You in like manner will reflect on and curse your folly. 5. The torment that those spoken of in the doctrine have endured for so long a time, is but an imprisonment in order to execution. "Spirits *in prison."* "Chains of darkness." There is another day. Their misery is great now. We learn by the parable of Dives and Lazarus....I know nothing by which it can be determined that the misery will not be a thousand times as great...They ‘tremble.’ The chains are strong. There is no hope...no relief to them. 6. The means and opportunities they enjoyed were nothing to yours. Little revelation of a future state. 7. God has an appointed time to wait on you. ‘The Spirit will not always strive.’ 8. Consider the sudden, unexpected manner in which they were destroyed (Luke xvii. 26, 27.)...You will probably be surprised as they were. They had as much reason to flatter themselves as you, (Job xxvii. 20)- "A tempest stealeth him away in the night."

Directions[^015-note2]

<!-- p. 208 -->

1. Avoid those things by which the men that went to Hell in Noah’s time undid themselves...particularly sensuality, (Genesis vi.; Luke xvii. 27.)...So it was with Sodom. Violence. "Evil imaginations." They would not hearken. They grew hardened to long-continued calls and warnings...habitually made light. Is there not reason to apprehend that this is the case with many here? You must reform your life...thorough reformation.... 2. You must in this respect be as Noah was in the generation of those souls that are "castaways;" particularly 1. You must avoid those liberties of young people that are customary. 2. Another thing that is apparently become customary, and doubtless is very provoking to God, is pride and extravagance is apparel. Not that I condemn all adorning the body. ’Tis evident by Scripture that some moderate degree of this is lawful. Oil that makes his face to shine, (Ecclesiastes ix. 8; Matthew vi. 17; Proverbs xxxi. 21, 22; Exodus iii 22.). But yet ’tis apparent that there is a most sinful extravagance in this kind, (1 Timothy ii. 9; 1 Peter iii. 3, 4; Isaiah iii. 16, 18, &c.) Appears to be very provoking to God...when persons go beyond their rank. One end of apparel seems to be to distinguish. (Proverbs xxxi. 22, 23.) Common people to shew an affectation to be like those of high rank; country towns to affect to be like the metropolis. When they go beyond their estate, disable themselves from paying their debts; deprive themselves of other things more necessary and more profitable; disable themselves much from deeds of charity. An affectation to distinguish themselves in imitating the fashions of the more gay part of the world. Complying with the general customs of a country in clothing is not vulgar. On the contrary, ’tis not decent to be singular. But some fashions in themselves are ill...extravagance...very costly...immodest... All this care, and pains, and cost to adorn themselves shews persons to much affect outward ornament...seem to shew that they make much of themselves...all that which tends to encourage a general excess. Such things as these have been condemned by wise men of all nations. ’Tis a time when the nations here have to a vast excess. The land is become exceedingly extravagant. More so than in England in proportion to our ability and ranks. Prevents great good that might be done...is continually running in debt. The main thing that brings our greatest national calamities...particularly the present state the country is in with regard to a medium.[^015-note3] source of that general injustice that has been so long complained of...keeps the country in constant distress...maintains constant injustice...threatens us with ruin....We in this town[^015-note4] are evidently got to a great excess. Boston is extravagant

<!-- p. 209 -->

beyond London. And we, considering all things, I think beyond them...how far below we fall short in rank...state... education and our situation in the world...far beyond them. I had occasion to observe the people at Portsmouth, in both the congregations in that place. That is a place much famed for politeness, and is a city much like Boston in many respects. I judged the apparel of our congregation was fully as costly. Many things that might make it proper for them to go beyond us. Such excess in gaiety and costliness of apparel in a manifestation of great vanity of mind. It seems to shew a great senselessness of our own vileness...becomes us to go modestly...modest apparel would better become such sinful creatures. Do not consider the end for which clothing was given...to hide our shame...that deformity that is the first of sin...senselessness of what our bodies are coming to...shews vanity of mind, as it shews the heart to be set on that which is exceeding vain and empty...great want of a sense of the worth of those spiritual ornaments that are infinitely more valuable. If persons had a proper concern for their soul, there surely would not appear very much affectation to... If religion should greatly revive it would undoubtedly make a great alteration. This is one thing, among others, that tends to prevent its reviving. God has of late awfully testified His displeasure for the extravagance of the country’s manner of living. 3. You must not only seek, but strive. You must make it a business...main business....thorough... How plain the necessity of it! How often the Word of God! How plain by experience! On many accounts necessary...How much you have had to convince you of the necessity! And are you, after all, in any sense awake? And how can you excuse this folly? 4. Be seasonable. The sinners of the old world perished by the Flood because they were not seasonable. They doubtless, many of them, used means afterwards. Had need to make haste....So we are directed from time to time. "Haste"..."escape for thy LIFE." On many accounts necessary. How many have we reason to think perish through delay! 5. Not only engage in but go through that great work that is necessary in order to your escaping eternal damnation. Do in this respect as Noah did for the saving himself and his house from the Flood. 6. Don’t waste time in halting between two opinions. How there seem to be some things that give special encouragement. Isaiah lv. 6, 7.

[^015-note1]: In left-hand corner, marked "June 1749." See ‘Directions’ for second sermon.-G.

[^015-note2]: Text and Doctrine placed here, shewing a second sermon commenced at thispoint.-G.

[^015-note3]: That is, "the currency" coinage.-G.

[^015-note4]: Viz., Northampton, New England.-G.

Jonathan Edwards

Lecture septem 1749.

_________

Isai 34. 4.

_____

something beyond their temporal Calamities which were by the Hand of men

___

1. what The thing here threatend in General viz that G. would take vengeance 2. The manner [^022-note1] of the vengeance 1. That G. would meet Them

_____

2. That He will not meet them as a man destruction of Babilon some Resemblance of the futu Gods future vengeance at the d. of J. Therefore often spoken of in language borrowed from them & accommated to the to that day of [^022-note2] wrath .

_______________________

doc. when Gods Time comes to take vengeance on his Enemies . He will not meet Them as a man. Prop. I. There is an appointed Time Time whe Prop. I There is [^022-note3] a certain Time ap pointed for G. to that G. hath appointed for the executing vengeance on his Enemies G. surely will take vengeance on his Enemies. as He says in the Text ---

__

The vengeance of the most high will deut. 32. 35. To me belongeth vengeance [^022-note4] deut. 7. 10. He will not be slack

____

Tis no sign to the Contrary that hitherto Gods Enemies seem to be let alone It was no sign to the Contrary that Babilon Reason shews That B Reason teaches --[^022-note5] that the Enemies of the most high cannot alwaies be let alone It nee must needs be contrary to --- disagreable to the [--] divine Perfection contrary to Inconsistent with the nature of things Inconsistent with the support di & dignity of the divine governour [^022-note6]

contrary to the Great established everlasting Rule of Righ. vengeance appointed in this Rule appointed [-] in Gods frequently declared purpose immutable decrees declared not meerly [^022-note7] in the form of a Threatning predictions the manner foretold & Consequences

_______________________

And as the Thing is appointed unalterable so the Time is appointed G. has appointed seasons [^022-note8]

____

we often read of the day. as much appointed & fixed & regularly & wisely as the periodical seasons measured out by the motion of the Heavenly Bodies The Reason why Let alone for the Present is ------ not yet come [^022-note9]

In general The day of death is a day of vengeance II. when the appointed [^022-note10] Time of vengeance G. will meet his Enemies. It may be for the Present . G. lets Them very much alone dont appear to Them hides Hims many of T & They are ready to think there is no G. Ps. 14 1. The Fool – no G. Ps. 50. 21. [^022-note11] others are ready to think G. is such an one as Thems. neglects Human affairs as Tho G. is Ignorant Job. 22. 13. How doth G. know can ---- can He Judge through the thick Cloud Thick Clouds are a Covering

Ps. 94. 7. They say the L. shall not see neither shall the G. of Jacob regard it. neglects human [^022-note12] affairs walketh in the Circuit of Heaven Ps. 94. 7. they say the L. shall not see neither shall the G. of Jacob Regard it G. seems to let them [^022-note13] alone & he dont meet Them. & They Let G. alone no talking to G. dont desire his Presence Choose to have nothing to do with Him dont go alone from Time to Time to meet with G. & if or if They do [^022-note14] for a while they soon grow wearyy of it choose to keep at a distance aliens & strangers

_______________________

But when the appointed Time -- Then G. will meet with them however unworthy & averse. & They must meet [^022-note15] G. wi with G. amos . 4. 12. Theref. thus will I do unto Thee O Israel & because I will this unto thee prepare to meet thy G. O Israel

___________

G. will appear to them shall see G. G. will have imme be sensibly in his immediate Presence fall into his Hands G. will deal with them In the day that I shall deal with Thee Thus it will be at [^022-note16] death. sp. returns to G. who Gave it

G. will meet his Enemies at the d. of Judgmt .

_________________ [^022-note17]

III . When G. shall thus meet his Enemies in the day of vengeance He will not meet Them as a man. e He The terrour & dreadful [ ] Effects with which He will meet them will not be like & the dreadful Consequences of the meeting shall not be like that whi with which men are ever wont to meet their Enemies He will not meet men have sometimes met their Enemies with [^022-note18]

[^022-note19]

great Terrour. [^022-note20] There have many men that have been distinguished for valour & Fortitude in war many great Heroes who have been very terribly in the war and it would strike has been enough to fill the Hearts of men their Enemies of with Terrour to think of meeting them in the Field of Batttle in their dreadful Armour in the Field of Battle But G when He comes to take vengeance on his Enemies wont meet Them like those Earthly[^022-note21] Heroes Heroes He wont meet Them as Alexander Pompey & Julius Cesar were wont to meet thei who conquered so many Nations & as th it were trod the world under foot were wont to meet their Enemies at the Head of their victorious Armies It seemed Terrible He wont meet as an Earthly King ready to the Battle or as a Giant. It appeared terrible to the Chil. of Is [^022-note22] to meet Goliath with his Helmet of Brass & Coat of mail weighing five Thous. shekels & his Greaves of Brass upon his Legs & a Forget of Brass upon his shoulders and his spear whose staff was like a weavers Beam saul King saul & his People were dismayed & g at the sight of Him & greatly afraid & fled from before Him But it will be more terrible to meet the Great G. when He comes forth as an [^022-note23] Enemy before whom all Kings & armies & Giants are lik as Grasshoppers & as Nothing & less than nothing & vanity.

________

when Earthly Princes have been greatly provoked it with any of their subjects it has been terrible to meet them or be brought into their Pres. Prov. 20. 2. The wrath of a King is as the Roring of a Lion --- But it will be terrible to em to meet the King [^022-note24] of Kings & be brought into his dreadf. Presence when He comes to take vengeance on his Enemies. when it is said in the Text I will not meet Thee as a man . There was There is something to be to be understand by it not only that which is plainly expressed but also that which is evi something that is evidently implied That which is expressed in that G. will meet his Enemies [^022-note25] as a man. & what is implied is that He will meet em as G.

______________________

I shall briefly consider both these 1 I would con

______

1 That which is expressed or that G. will not not meet [^022-note26] Them as a man But in a very diverse manner . & He will do so in the following Respects. 1 He will not meet [^022-note27] Them with such displays & vindi cations of majesty & authority as men that Earthly Rulers or with which earthly rulers those that provoke Them . nebuchad Earth The Authority & majesty of Earthly many Earthly Rulers is Guarded with Terrible Punishmt [^022-note28]

Nebuchadnezzar . 2. Not meet [^022-note29] Them with such manifesta. of for strength as men that wherewith men . Heroes. sampson Giants sampson. many men have [^022-note30] Greatly exerted their strength to make their wrath Terrible 3. Not with the [^022-note31] Mutability of a man. Those weak Chan geable Passions tha men Changeable fo strength precarious strength. Precarious men that mutable decaying Life men who have fallen into the Hands of their Enraged fellow creatures have [^022-note32] often been delivered by their death. Isai 51. 12. who art Thou – afraid of man that shall die

a possibility of Escape

(2) what is evidently implied is -- meet as a G. with agreable to the Great End of all things agreable to Gods special design in his moral Governmt shew his Love shew his wrath [^022-note35] with displays with proper displays of the majesty & authority of G. a G.

strength of a G. shew make his Power Known. [^022-note36]

The Eternity of a G. Application. [^022-note38] Use of Exhortation to those who have hitherto remained Enemies to G. to make their Peace with Him I speak to all that are in a natural state for all men by Nature are Enemies to G. they are under the Power of a carnal mind that is Enmity ---- It is indeed a dreadful Enemy that you have to deal with for while you remain an Enemy to G. you make Him your Enemy & his wrath abides on [^022-note39] you that is verified concerning all in your Condition in Hos 11. 8 my soul loathed them & their soul abhorred me me you and th a poor mortal sinful Crea ture and the great Jehovah are opposite one to the other & the match is in finitely unequal ‘tis not that of a Kid against a Lamb or Kid a against a Lion. nor little Child against a strong ma an armed man nor is of a Kid against a [^022-note40] Lion or an Infant against a Giant or a little worm swelling & exerting it self against Leviathan but it is a worm of weak worm against the Infinite God that made Heaven & Earth and has the whole Universe every moment in his Hands

_______

many sinners do oppose this do set Thems in oppo sit. to this Great & dredfull being with great Boldness indeed. [^022-note41] They provoke the L. to Jealousy as tho They thought They were stronger than He as tho They doubted not but they should be able to make their Part Good with Him Job. [-] 15. 25, 26. For He stretcheth out his Hand against G. & strengtheth Him self against the Almighty He runneth on Him even on his neck upon the thick Bosses of this Bucklers & thus it is with [^022-note42] you if you are an unawakened sinner one & one that neglects the great your souls concern & live in the neglect of secret Prayer & other ways of Known sin you are one of those bold presumptuous Enemies of the most high you oppose G. & fight against & act the Part of an Enemy to Him without Fear while G. seems to you to be at a distance[^022-note43] But the Time will soon come when G. will not longer be seem at a distance Hell come to you & you must meet Him & He will not meet you as a man then it will appear how unequal the match is between a little venomous worm & the infinit nite Jehovah then it will soon be seen whether you can make your Part Good with Him or no & whether you [^022-note44] had Cause for such security bol & boldness in sinning against G. then it will be seen what becomes of your stoutness of Heart & that Presumption & fear lessness of sp. with which you appe lived in opposi tion to G. Then will that be fulfilled concerning you . Job. 15. 24. Trouble & Anguish shall come upon Him make Him afraid They shall Prevail [^022-note45] against Him as a King ready to the Battle . -------

--------

Then Trembling & amazemt shall take hold upon your Heart in a moment will become like wax --- in the midst ------ Isai 33. Can he can Thine Hea Ezek. 22. 14 Can thine Hands be strong or can thine Heart endure in the day that I shall deal with Thee I the L. have spoken alas Alas to [^022-note46] what purpose will it now you oppose & as it were boldly confront the most high now he lets you alone but then you would Gladly flee – -- Job. 27. 20, 21, 22

---------------------

-----------

you would flee willingly hide your self in the Caves & holes of the Earth & Holes of the Rock [^022-note47]

But there will be no fleeing there will be no escaping & what will be come of you when this Time comes as it will soon come if you repent not your dredful Enemy hath whet his Glistning sword his he hath made ready his Arrows vengeance is prepared & your damnation dont slumber . G is not asleep nor is his wrath asleep He [^022-note48] has you in his Hands every moment & tho yo & tho you seem for the present to be let alone yet there is no danger of your Getting out of his Reach . And is n when his Time it will appear that He is a Consuming & that all your strenth is but as Ch stubble and Chaff before the Flames & as Chaff in a furnace the midst of a furnace [^022-note49]

& what will you do when the omnipotent Jehovah shall Cloth Hims. with his Great Power rise up to meet you as your Enemy & shall be Put on the Garmts of ven geance & shall ex with a design to Let all m Let all see what the wrath of and vengeance of a G. & when ye & when shall call on all near & far off to take notice & see what He does . & take notice of the Lif wonderful displays [^022-note50] of his Infinite awful majesty & in unparalleld Power Isai 33. 12, 13. [-] Hear ye that are far off . Consider Truly it is a fearfulo thing to fall into the Hands of the living G.

you have it may be thought G. was altogether such an one as your self . But All the SS. Representa tions of Gods future vengeance on his Enemies seem to shew that G. will wonderfully [^022-note51] shew his Power & his majesty . ------

1. whither X was going 2. His forwardness in going 3. How his behaviour[^023-note1] 3. at what Time this was 2 3. what were like to as be the Consequences of this his Conduct thus going to Jes Jerusalem at this Time 3 X X was sensible of those Consequences 3 His forwardness in going 4.
